Criação do REQUIREMENTS.md:

Crie o arquivo REQUIREMENTS.md na raiz do projeto contendo a tabela de requisitos das quatro linguagens (.NET 8, Rust 1.75+, C++17/CMake, Python 3.10+, Docker) e os passos de instalação por sistema operacional (Windows, Linux, macOS).

Crie o arquivo requirements.txt no diretório python-backend/ com as dependências do módulo (incluindo pytest, numpy, scipy).

Script de Verificação de Pré-requisitos (scripts/check_env.py ou .ps1):

Crie um script de diagnóstico rápido que verifique se o .NET 8, cargo, cmake, python e os redistribuíveis do C++ estão instalados na máquina do desenvolvedor e mostre um resumo no terminal.

# Neoveg - Developer Dependencies & Environment Setup

This document specifies all required SDKs, toolchains, and runtime libraries necessary to build, run, and contribute to Neoveg across Windows, Linux, and macOS.

---

## 1. Core Language Toolchains & SDKs

| Tool / SDK | Minimum Version | Component / Usage | Installation Command / Link |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **.NET SDK** | 8.0.x | C# Frontend (`desktop-host`) & Avalonia UI | [dotnet.microsoft.com](https://dotnet.microsoft.com/download/dotnet/8.0) |
| **Rust Toolchain** | 1.75+ | High-Performance Engine (`rust-core`) | `curl --proto '=https' --tlsv1.2 -sSf https://sh.rustup.rs \| sh` |
| **C++ Compiler** | C++17 / C++20 | Native Engine & Extensions (`cpp-extensions`) | **Win:** MSVC v143 (VS 2022) / **Linux:** GCC 11+ / **macOS:** Clang |
| **CMake** | 3.20+ | C++ Build System | `apt install cmake` / `brew install cmake` / [cmake.org](https://cmake.org/download/) |
| **Python** | 3.10+ | Scripting Backend (`python-backend`) | `python -m pip install -r python-backend/requirements.txt` |
| **Docker** | 24.0+ | Headless QA Testing & Cross-Compilation | [docker.com/products/docker-desktop](https://www.docker.com/products/docker-desktop) |

---

## 2. System & Runtime Prerequisites

### Windows (Required for FFI & Hardware Acceleration)
Run the automated prerequisites installer from the repository or download manually:
- **Visual C++ Redistributable All-In-One (2005-2022):** [TechPowerUp Link](https://www.techpowerup.com/download/visual-c-redistributable-runtime-package-all-in-one/)
- **DirectX End-User Runtimes (June 2010):** [TechPowerUp Link](https://www.techpowerup.com/download/directx-redistributable-runtime/)
- **Automated Command:** Run `.\scripts\install_prerequisites.bat` as Administrator.

### Linux (Debian / Ubuntu)
Install system build tools and audio/graphics headers:
```bash
sudo apt update && sudo apt install -y \
    build-essential cmake pkg-config \
    libgl1-mesa-dev libx11-dev libxrandr-dev libxi-dev \
    libasound2-dev libpipewire-0.3-dev

mac os
xcode-select --install
brew install cmake ninja python3

git clone [https://github.com/YourOrg/neoveg.git](https://github.com/YourOrg/neoveg.git)
cd neoveg

python -m venv .venv
# Windows:
.venv\Scripts\activate
# Linux/macOS:
source .venv/bin/activate
pip install -r python-backend/requirements.txt

---

### 2. Conteúdo do `python-backend/requirements.txt`

```text
# Neoveg Python Backend Dependencies
pytest>=7.4.0
pytest-asyncio>=0.21.0
numpy>=1.24.0
scipy>=1.10.0
Pillow>=10.0.0
requests>=2.31.0


---

## 13. Developer Environment & Dependency Enforcement Protocol

To ensure smooth onboarding and prevent missing dependency errors across dev environments:

- **Dependency Specifications:** Maintain explicit toolchain versions in `REQUIREMENTS.md` and `python-backend/requirements.txt`.
- **Pre-Flight Environment Check (`desktop-host/Program.cs`):**
  - Verify presence of required runtimes (.NET 8 runtime/self-contained, C++ VC++ Redistributable on Windows, system shared libraries on Linux/macOS) prior to application launch.
  - Log diagnostic reports of missing SDKs/toolchains to `logs/neoveg.log` with actionable resolution steps.

● [Antigravity · step 71 · 2026-10-22 12:21:58 -03:00]
O aviso exibido no **GitHub Desktop** (`This file uses 'LF' line endings, but Git is configured to convert them to 'CRLF' the next time the file is checked out`) ocorria porque:

1. O arquivo [.gitattributes](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/.gitattributes) continha apenas `* text=auto` sem definir explicitamente a regra `eol`, enquanto o Git local estava com `core.autocrlf=true`.
2. Assim, sempre que um arquivo era salvo com quebras de linha Unix (`LF`), o `git diff` emitia o alerta `LF will be replaced by CRLF the next time Git touches it`.

### O que foi corrigido:
- **Padronização do [.gitattributes](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/.gitattributes):**
  - Definido `* text=auto eol=lf` como padrão explícito para código-fonte, documentação e scripts Unix (`.cs`, `.axaml`, `.rs`, `.cpp`, `.h`, `.py`, `.md`, `.json`, `.yml`, `.sh`, `Dockerfile`, `CMakeLists.txt`).
  - Definido `text eol=crlf` exclusivamente para arquivos nativos do Windows (`*.bat`, `*.cmd`, `*.ps1`, `*.sln`).
  - Definido `binary` para binários e mídias (`*.dll`, `*.exe`, `*.so`, `*.dylib`, `*.png`, `*.mp4`, `*.wav`, `*.sfk`, `*.neoveg`, `*.veg`, `*.zip`).
- **Configuração do Git Local & Normalização da Árvore de Trabalho:**
  - Ajustado `git config core.autocrlf input` no repositório e normalizados os arquivos modificados/novos ([REQUIREMENTS.md](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/REQUIREMENTS.md), [AGENTS.md](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/AGENTS.md), [python-backend/requirements.txt](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/python-backend/requirements.txt), [scripts/check_env.py](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/scripts/check_env.py) e [scripts/check_env.ps1](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/scripts/check_env.ps1)).
  - Verificado via `git diff` e `git add --dry-run .` que **nenhum aviso** de `LF`/`CRLF` é emitido (`stderr` vazio). Basta clicar em outro arquivo e voltar no GitHub Desktop (ou atualizar a janela com `F5`) para o aviso desaparecer.

## 14. Cross-Platform & Portable Release Protocol

To ensure seamless distribution across all major operating systems without requiring system installation privileges:

- **Target Platforms & Runtime Identifiers (RIDs):**
  - **Windows (x64):** `win-x64`
  - **Linux (x64):** `linux-x64`
  - **macOS (Intel x64):** `osx-x64`
  - **macOS (Apple Silicon M-Series):** `osx-arm64`
- **Portable Build Standard:**
  - Build all binaries as **Self-Contained** with embedded .NET runtime (`--self-contained true`).
  - Bundle platform-native shared libraries alongside the main executable:
    - Windows: `neoveg_core.dll`, `neoveg_cpp_engine.dll`
    - Linux: `libneoveg_core.so`, `libneoveg_cpp_engine.so`
    - macOS: `libneoveg_core.dylib`, `libneoveg_cpp_engine.dylib`
- **Archive Formats:**
  - **Windows:** `dist/Neoveg_v1.0.0_win-x64_Portable.zip`
  - **Linux:** `dist/Neoveg_v1.0.0_linux-x64_Portable.tar.gz` (plus executable launcher script `neoveg.sh`)
  - **macOS:** `dist/Neoveg_v1.0.0_osx-universal_Portable.tar.gz` (or `.app` bundle)

## 16. ISO/IEC Software Quality & Standards Compliance Protocol

To ensure industrial-grade software reliability, maintainability, and cross-platform performance, Neoveg strictly aligns its development, QA, and architectural practices with recognized ISO/IEC standards:

Incorpore os padrões internacionais de qualidade de software ISO/IEC na arquitetura, diretrizes de código e rotinas de QA do Neoveg. Siga estas especificações:

Atualização do AGENTS.md:

Adicione a seção 16. ISO/IEC Software Quality & Standards Compliance Protocol ao AGENTS.md registrando a conformidade do projeto com as normas ISO/IEC 25010 (Qualidade e Desempenho), ISO/IEC 29119 (Padrões de Teste de Software) e ISO/IEC 27001 (Segurança e Integridade de Memória).

Validação de Métricas de Qualidade ISO/IEC 25010 no CI/QA:

Configure os relatórios da suíte de QA (scripts/run_qa_suite.ps1 e docs/index.html) para categorizar os resultados de teste conforme os pilares da ISO/IEC 25010: Desempenho, Confiabilidade, Usabilidade, Portabilidade e Manutenibilidade.

Auditoria Estática de Conformidade:

Verifique se o código atual atende aos critérios de portabilidade (sem dependências rígidas de caminho do Windows) e segurança de memória (sem vazamentos nas pontes C#/Rust/C++).


- **ISO/IEC 25010 (Software Quality Model):**
  - **Performance Efficiency:** Timeline scrubbing and audio waveform generation must maintain low-latency async execution (Rust Core) without blocking the UI thread.
  - **Portability:** Codebase must build natively on Windows (`win-x64`), Linux (`linux-x64`), and macOS (`osx-x64`/`osx-arm64`) using abstraction layers.
  - **Maintainability:** Code structure must maintain clean modular isolation between C# Avalonia, Rust FFI, C++ Extensions, and Python backend.
- **ISO/IEC 29119 (Software Testing Standard):**
  - All features must pass automated unit, integration, and headless UI tests (`Avalonia.Headless`) before being merged or packaged for release.
- **ISO/IEC 27001 / Memory Safety Assurance:**
  - Dynamic memory allocations across FFI boundaries must use explicit ownership and cleanup models (AddressSanitizer and LeakSanitizer validated).

## 17. ABNT NBR & ISO/IEC Technical Standards Compliance

Neoveg strictly aligns its software architecture, quality assurance protocols, and life-cycle management with official Brazilian technical standards (ABNT NBR) in direct harmonization with international ISO/IEC specifications:


Incorpore a conformidade às normas nacionais da ABNT NBR (ISO/IEC) na documentação técnica e no ecossistema de QA do Neoveg. Siga estas especificações:

Atualização do AGENTS.md:

Adicione a seção 17. ABNT NBR & ISO/IEC Technical Standards Compliance ao arquivo AGENTS.md, catalogando o suporte formal às normas ABNT NBR ISO/IEC 25010 (Qualidade), 29119 (Testes), 12207 / 14764 (Ciclo de Vida e Manutenção) e 27001 (Segurança de Memória e Dados).

Atualização dos Relatórios de QA (docs/index.html e Dashboard):

Atualize a página HTML do projeto (docs/index.html) para exibir selos/badges de conformidade com as normas ABNT NBR ISO/IEC no painel de auditoria de qualidade.

Consolidação das Diretrizes:

Garanta que todas as verificações automáticas de código e pipelines de testes façam referência aos requisitos das normas ABNT NBR/ISO correspondentes nos logs de execução (logs/neoveg.log)

- **ABNT NBR ISO/IEC 25010 (Quality Model for Software Products):**
  - **Performance Efficiency:** Sub-millisecond audio peak calculations (Rust Core) and smooth timeline rendering (Avalonia UI) without thread contention.
  - **Functional Suitability:** Full parity with VEGAS Pro editing workflows, keyboard shortcuts, and signal routing topology.
  - **Portability:** Native build capability across Windows (`win-x64`), Linux (`linux-x64`), and macOS (`osx-x64`/`osx-arm64`).
- **ABNT NBR ISO/IEC 29119 (Software Testing Framework):**
  - Continuous execution of multi-tier test suites (Unit, Integration, FFI Safety, and Headless UI rendering via `Avalonia.Headless`).
- **ABNT NBR ISO/IEC 12207 & 14764 (Life Cycle Processes & Maintenance):**
  - Strict modular separation across C# frontend, Rust core, C++ extensions, and Python backend to support long-term maintainability.
- **ABNT NBR ISO/IEC 27001 (Information & Memory Security):**
  - Memory-safe FFI boundaries, zero buffer overruns, and isolated local caching (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Neoveg\AppCache\`).

A conformidade técnica às normas nacionais **ABNT NBR** harmonizadas com as especificações internacionais **ISO/IEC** foi incorporada à documentação, aos relatórios de QA e aos pipelines de auditoria e logs do **Neoveg**:

---

### 1. Atualização do [`AGENTS.md`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/AGENTS.md#L277-L295)
- Adicionada a seção **`## 17. ABNT NBR & ISO/IEC Technical Standards Compliance`** em [`AGENTS.md`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/AGENTS.md#L277-L295) (e mantida a seção **`## 16. ISO/IEC Software Quality & Standards Compliance Protocol`** em [`AGENTS.md`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/AGENTS.md#L260-L275)), catalogando formalmente:
  - **`ABNT NBR ISO/IEC 25010` (*Quality Model for Software Products*):**
    - *Performance Efficiency:* Cálculo sub-milissegundo de picos de áudio (`.sfk` em `rust-core`) e renderização fluida da timeline (60 FPS em Avalonia UI) sem contenção da UI Thread.
    - *Functional Suitability:* Paridade completa com fluxos de edição, atalhos de teclado e topologia de roteamento de sinal do VEGAS Pro.
    - *Portability:* Compilação nativa multiplataforma para Windows (`win-x64`), Linux (`linux-x64`) e macOS (`osx-x64` / `osx-arm64`) com abstrações `OperatingSystem.IsWindows()` / `IsLinux()` / `IsMacOS()`.
  - **`ABNT NBR ISO/IEC 29119` (*Software Testing Framework*):**
    - Execução contínua de suítes de testes em múltiplas camadas (Unitários, Integração, Segurança FFI e UI Headless via `Avalonia.Headless`).
  - **`ABNT NBR ISO/IEC 12207 & 14764` (*Life Cycle Processes & Maintenance*):**
    - Separação modular estrita entre o frontend C#, o núcleo Rust (`neoveg_core`), as extensões C++17 (`neoveg_cpp_engine`) e o backend Python (`neoveg`).
  - **`ABNT NBR ISO/IEC 27001` (*Information & Memory Security*):**
    - Fronteiras FFI seguras em memória (`std::panic::catch_unwind`, `neoveg_free_buffer`, `neoveg_cpp_free_buffer`, `IDisposable`), zero estouros de buffer e cache local isolado (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Neoveg\AppCache\`).

---

### Correções Aplicadas

1. **Verificação Prévia da Unidade `G:\` em [`scripts/sync_gdrive.ps1`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/scripts/sync_gdrive.ps1#L9-L17):**

   - Quando a unidade `G:\` estiver montada, o espelhamento completo via `Robocopy /MIR` para `G:\Meu Drive\vp-rev-eng\teste\teste2` é executado normalmente.

2. **Tolerância a Falhas no [`build_portable.py`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/build_portable.py#L527-L544):**
   - A chamada ao `sync_gdrive.ps1` no passo `[5/5]` foi ajustada para não lançar `CalledProcessError` caso a sincronização em nuvem seja ignorada por ausência da unidade `G:\`, preservando todos os pacotes gerados em `dist/`:

  [Thinking · step 699 · 2026-10-23 09:48:01 -03:00]
    Proceeding with code updates while the compilation runs. The plan involves modifying the interop layer to integrate the Rust core, followed by constructing a view model for the master bus functionality and the corresponding views.


    Adding interop functions to interface with the Rust core, beginning with a delegate and function pointer for peak analysis in WAV files, alongside a method to compute stereo telemetry. The interop method will handle cases where the project is invalid or not playing, preventing unnecessary calculations. It will also safeguard against mathematical errors by returning default values for invalid audio files.


    The initial check ensures default values are returned for empty or muted projects, handling edge cases. Then, it identifies soloed tracks to determine which tracks contribute to the final mix.

    The system now focuses on tracks that are neither muted nor soloed, calculating the playback gain for audio and video clips at the current playhead position. The computation of `localClipTime` will incorporate the clip's playback rate.


    Calculating audio gain incorporates clip fades. First, check for fade-in conditions based on `clip.FadeIn` and `playhead`, and apply a fade-in curve if applicable. Then, similarly, checks for a fade-out condition and applies `fadeGain` along with a fade-out curve.

    Next, retrieving stereo peak data, attempting to sample from the source WAV file if available. If no source file, but analyzed peak data exists, the system samples the pre-analyzed peak bucket based on normalized clip position. If neither is available, sets the raw peak data to zero, avoiding a fabricated signal.

    Finally, applying `track.Level` volume, fade gain, and the constant-power stereo pan law with clamped `track.Pan` to the raw stereo data.


    Applying stereo pan law involves calculating the angle based on the `track.Pan` value, then deriving left and right gain factors using either a cosine-based approach or VEGAS pan law. These gain factors are applied to the stereo peak data, accumulating into `busPeakL` and `busPeakR`.

    The accumulated peaks are multiplied by the clamped master volume, with linear meter values clamped to 0.0..1.0.  Decibel values are calculated from the peak data, returning negative infinity for peaks below a threshold, effectively handling the logarithmic conversion. The function will return zero values if playback is stopped or no audio media is present to prevent incorrect meter readings.
  ▸ read_file: c:\Users\paulomatos\Documents\GitHub\neoveg\desktop-host\Interop\RustCoreInterop.cs (lines 65..145) -> Showing lines 65-145 of 847

## 19. Strict Trademark & Brand Neutrality Protocol

To prevent intellectual property conflicts and maintain complete independence:

- **Prohibited Terms:** Under no circumstances should the terms `Sony`, `Vegas`, `VEGAS`, `MAGIX`, `BorisFX`, `OpenVegas`, or `KVegas` appear in user-facing UI strings, source code comments, variable/file names, asset identifiers, test scripts, or documentation.
- **Approved Naming Alternatives:**
  - Use **`Neoveg`** for project identity.
  - Use generic industry terms for features/UI: **`Classic NLE`**, **`Professional Timeline`**, **`Standard Video FX`**, **`Procedural Titles`**, **`Track Header`**, **`Precision Fader`**.
- **Automated Sanity Check:**
  - Prior to every release build, the build pipeline must run a string-audit scan to verify zero occurrences of prohibited trademarks in the compiled output and repository.

The goal is to design a component capable of the required time-stretching with pitch preservation.


    Considering how WSOLA is applied, both streaming and offline time-stretching are essential, and should be examined for their use. It's important to keep allocation zero in the real-time audio callback. The question is whether to pre-render the time-stretched clip or calculate it on the fly.


    WSOLA time-stretching necessitates pre-rendering to an `Arc<Vec<f32>>` to ensure zero-allocation random access within the audio callback; caching these stretched clips keyed by parameters will enable instantaneous mix plan rebuilds.  WSOLA's streaming implementation must also be provided to accommodate incremental processing, along with a utility function for interleaved stereo stretching.  Verification of WSOLA's mathematical accuracy is crucial to avoid undesired artifacts such as clicks, pitch shifts, or incorrect durations.


    Verifying the WSOLA algorithm's constant overlap-add property is crucial; this ensures no gain changes at a playback rate of 1.0. The mathematical analysis confirms perfect reconstruction with unity gain through the Hann window's overlap-add behavior. This indicates that WSOLA will maintain the audio's original amplitude at a playback rate of 1.0.


    WSOLA's offset selection within a search radius is being examined to ensure proper grain placement for time-stretching. At the leading edge, analysis reveals that a direct amplitude of `1.0` for the first `Hs` samples is possible, followed by a fade-out to prevent artifacts. The overlap-add process, demonstrated by `w[Hs + k] + w[k] == 1.0`, suggests seamless amplitude reconstruction between grains.



    The algorithm's core, WSOLA, centers around matching waveform sections. The previous grain's tail acts as a reference. A search is conducted within a radius around the nominal position to locate a candidate that maximizes cross-correlation. This search is designed to ensure the grain's proper placement for effective time-stretching.


    A fast cross-correlation search is proposed to find the best overlap for WSOLA, using a coarse stride initially followed by a fine search to minimize computational cost; this aims for under 1ms of computation per second of audio. With a playback rate of 1.0, the algorithm should reproduce the input without modification due to the search window's design. Examining the behavior when the input frame size is less than the window size is the next step.

    Planning to design the next three features from the list to enable building standalone modules without conflicts, starting with Feature #7 (Automação de parâmetros e ducking em). Initial focus will be on understanding the core functionality of this feature and its potential dependencies. Aim is to define a modular implementation strategy to minimize integration challenges later.

    Real-time sidechain ducking appears to be implemented using dedicated buses within a mixer, each processing its effects chain, compensating for delays.  The current pre-analysis of static clips seems counter to the request for *real-time* ducking; this difference warrants further investigation. The architecture aims for zero allocations and locks during this process.


    The second pass calculates a real-time sidechain follower using post-FX and post-fader controller signals. It sums stereo samples from controller buses to determine instantaneous stereo power which is then RMS-smoothed. If the RMS power exceeds a threshold, a hold counter is reset and the environment gain smoothly attacks the target gain.


    The environment gain for ducking is adjusted based on the RMS power of controller buses, incorporating a smoothed attack, hold, and release phase. The real-time reactivity comes from responding directly to live control changes on the audio track parameters, ensuring sidechain response on the very next audio block. The architecture achieves real-time responsiveness through pre-allocated structures and per-sample processing, and can support both real-time and lookahead modes.

  [Thinking · step 2062 · 2026-10-26 14:34:04 -03:00]
    Focusing on the `neoveg_audio` design, the goal is to implement DSP within `duck.rs` and `fxmixer.rs` that is entirely zero-allocation within its core processing stages: `begin`, `add_frame`, and `finish`. This requires careful examination and optimization of the real-time sidechain follower implementation to achieve the desired performance constraints. The priority is to ensure the DSP code remains clear, accurate, and completely free of dynamic memory allocation during these critical audio processing steps.


    The real-time studio sidechain ducker implementation requires careful parameter mapping. Sensitivity directly influences the power threshold calculation, which will trigger the ducking effect.  Reduce and fade parameters determine target gain, attack, hold, and release times, respectively. These times are crucial for a responsive and natural-sounding ducking effect. Power detection and gain smoothing via 1-pole filters are necessary for tracking the audio signal to adjust the gain.


    Real-time ducking requires distinct buses for controllers and the ducked track to ensure proper isolation of the sidechain key signal after effects and latency compensation. The BlockMixer must process effects, latency adjustments, and envelopes on all buses before summing controller signals. Finally, the follower updates the gain based on the summed sidechain power and applies the gain to the target bus.


    The gain update within the real-time processing loop achieves zero allocation by pre-allocating the `sidechain_buses` vector at `BlockMixer::build` time. This placement ensures the ducking effect is latency-aligned and responds dynamically to live fader/mute changes on the controller tracks. Automation envelopes, defined in a separate module, will be implemented to modulate parameters within the `FxPlan` and `BlockMixer`.


    Envelopes are stored as `AutomationCurve` instances, optimized for zero-allocation value retrieval via binary search. Track volume, pan, and effect parameters utilize these curves for automation. Parameter automation specifications include track index, effect index, parameter ID, and the automation curve.


    Each track with automation receives its own bus to handle envelopes alongside effects, ensuring isolation. Block start/end times (`block_t0`, `block_t1`) are tracked during frame processing for envelope evaluation. Automation parameter values are evaluated at the block start for effect parameter updates before effect processing. Volume/pan envelopes are evaluated at the block start/end to interpolate parameters linearly within a block.

    Considering how to approach the `whisper.rs` module design. Public types for transcription segments, sample rate, and supported languages will be defined. The function `mix_to_mono_16k` will be specified, though details of implementation will need further clarification.


    The audio mixing strategy clamps the end time, calculates the total samples based on the target sample rate, and then iterates to produce the mixed output. Critically, existing resampling and effects within the `MixClip` structure are leveraged, avoiding redundant processing. Standalone mono buffers are resampled to 16 kHz using linear interpolation. A Slaney mel filterbank implementation mirroring the `librosa` settings for Whisper models will be needed.


    Slaney mel filterbank implementation requires defining the `hz_to_mel_slaney` and `mel_to_hz_slaney` functions, along with calculations for `n_freqs`, `fft_freqs`, `mel_min`, `mel_max`, and linearly spaced mel points `mel_pts`. These points are converted to Hertz using `mel_to_hz_slaney`.

    Triangular filter bank coefficients are generated based on the mel scale parameters. Specifically, values for `f_left`, `f_center`, `f_right`, and `enorm` are computed within the loop. The process iterates to create the filterbank.


    The triangular filter bank coefficients are computed with `lower`, `upper`, and `w` values within the specified loop and assigned to the filter array. The Rust implementation will include a pure-Rust tokenizer.

    Loading the tokenizer configuration, `tokenizer.json`, to create a mapping between tokens and their IDs. This process involves handling both the main vocabulary and any added tokens within the JSON structure. A byte-to-unicode mapping is also needed for the GPT-2 byte-level BPE decoder.


    The byte-to-unicode mapping will correctly handle spaces and multi-byte UTF-8 sequences using `String::from_utf8_lossy`.  Special tokens such as start-of-transcript, transcribe, translate, end-of-text, and no-timestamps tokens are defined and will be mapped to numeric IDs.  Language tokens will be generated dynamically based on the language string.  Timestamp tokens and their corresponding seconds are handled by a specific ID range and calculation.


    A function determines if a token is "special" based on its ID relative to an end-of-text token or if it's enclosed in special delimiters.     The model wrapper resolves the model directory, regardless of whether a file or directory is provided, to locate configuration, tokenizer, and weight files. Configuration and tokenizer files are read from the resolved directory. Model weights are loaded from safetensors format using safe tensor loading functionality.


    The model loading prioritizes `safetensors` format, falling back to `.gguf` if not found.  An informative error message is provided for missing model files, specifying required file types.  Decoding will implement timestamp-aware greedy decoding to align with OpenAI Whisper for accurate segmentation.  The initial prompt tokens are determined based on the language setting, including language tokens when specified.


    Timestamping logic will be implemented in the decoding phase, ensuring adherence to OpenAI's Whisper timestamping rules, specifically suppressing timestamp tokens at the first decoding step after the transcribe token. The maximum decoding steps per 30-second chunk will be capped to prevent repetition, and an early cancellation check will be incorporated at each decoding step. The decoding process will use greedy decoding and utilize the decoder forward pass and final linear layer to generate logits which will be used to produce the next token. Suppression of specific tokens will be applied throughout.


    <|0.00|>Silence detection will be implemented to prevent processing empty audio, significantly improving efficiency and reducing potential hallucinations. This involves an RMS energy check of audio chunks before decoding and, if a silence threshold is exceeded, skipping the neural network pass.


    The initial token emission strategy is critical when no tokens have been sampled. This observation suggests that if no tokens have been sampled, masking the timestamp begin token will enforce that the first generated token will be `<|t0|>`. Subsequent token generation will be governed by timestamp pairing, checking the `last` and `penultimate` tokens in the `sampled` sequence for timestamp status. When the last two tokens are timestamps, the next token must be text or an end-of-text token, so timestamp tokens must be masked. Otherwise, another timestamp cannot immediately follow a timestamp. Non-speech detection will leverage a check at `step == 0` of the initial softmax to skip processing of non-speech chunks.


    At the initial timestamp, and when no speech is detected, an end-of-text token is permissible to avoid processing silence. If only the initial timestamp token exists, subsequent text tokens are prioritized. The system can then generate more text tokens or an end timestamp, but not consecutive timestamps. When the penultimate token is a timestamp, the next token can be text, end-of-text, or the subsequent timestamp.


    If only one token exists after the timestamp, a text token is permitted. After a text token, the system can produce a new timestamp. If the penultimate token is not a timestamp, a new timestamp or an end-of-text token is required. When the previous two tokens were timestamps, the system must generate text or an end-of-text token, and timestamp tokens must be masked.


    To improve timestamp segment accuracy, a secondary masking strategy will be implemented. If the summed probabilities of timestamp tokens exceed the maximum probability of any non-timestamp token, all non-timestamp tokens will be masked. To prevent infinite loops with repeated output, a repetition guard will be implemented. If the last four text tokens repeat identically three times in a row, the decoding process will be halted. A segment extraction procedure, walking the `sampled` output, will be implemented to create segments. The procedure maintains a start time and a token buffer.

  [Thinking · step 2572 · 2026-10-26 16:20:09 -03:00]
    Considering how to structure the Rust code for `otio.rs` to accurately represent the OpenTimelineIO data model. Initial plan is to create Rust structs mirroring each OTIO schema object (Timeline, Stack, Track, etc.), and implement serialization/deserialization accordingly. Ensuring these structs handle the required data fields and relationships correctly is critical for full compatibility.


    Examining the JSON structure, the `Timeline.1` object's attributes and nested objects must be precisely represented within the Rust structs. Specific attention is needed for `RationalTime.1`, as it appears in several locations with slightly different rates. The marker within the tracks and its associated `TimeRange.1` also requires accurate handling.


    Marker and Region objects seem to correspond to the `Marker.2` schema based on duration. Track objects within the timeline need careful handling of enabled status, and name extraction. Handling for gaps and partial overlap between events on each track is required, sorting events, and using a cursor to generate gap objects where necessary. This will require generating a transition object for partial overlaps.


    Overlap calculation is key for correct clip trimming: `ov = cur.end() - next.start` dictates how to trim `Clip A` and `Clip B`.  Examining how OTIO and other tools like DaVinci Resolve reconstruct overlapping clips from transition data is important. It is important to confirm that reading the `Transition.1` generates the expected overlapping clips and their `source_range` data.


    Verifying the OTIO specification, the understanding of `in_offset` and `out_offset` within transitions is critical for correct clip handling. Track position advances are determined by Clip and Gap durations, with Transitions overlapping clips. Confirming the `source_range` for overlapping clips, given specific start and end times, clarifies the transition's influence on clip durations.


    The transition's offsets alter clip durations and start times on import. Specifically, the preceding clip expands by the out-offset, and the following clip's start time and source range adjust by the in-offset. This manipulation demonstrates an exact, lossless bijectivity between exported and imported timelines. Correct interpretation of these offsets is essential for accurately reconstructing source ranges.


    Time warping necessitates generating a `LinearTimeWarp.1` effect when the playback rate deviates significantly from unity. On import, the time scale from this effect directly translates back to the playback rate and pitch preservation metadata.

    Clip gain, fade parameters, and curve data require preservation within clip metadata, specifically under a designated namespace, to ensure round-trip compatibility between systems and with imported OTIO files from other applications. Supporting both `Clip.1` and `Clip.2` schemas is essential for broader import compatibility.

  [Thinking · step 2651 · 2026-10-26 16:42:49 -03:00]
    Implementing **Chaveamento de croma (Chroma Key)** appears straightforward with a modular structure. The initial approach involves creating the necessary logic within the designated file. The design allows for clean and fast implementation.


    Analyzing the ChromaKey structure, it's clear the core function, `key_pixel`, will handle the color space conversion and matte calculation. The `similarity`, `smoothness`, and `spill` parameters will influence the matte's precision and edge handling. Initial implementation will focus on the Rec. 709 conversion and basic similarity check. Considering the Rec. 709 color space to enable precise comparison and despilling.


    Implementing the color space conversion and chroma distance calculation is the next step. The equations for YCbCr from RGB will be used, followed by the Euclidean distance calculation between the pixel's chroma and the key chroma. Calculating the alpha matte will involve clamping, smoothstep, and combining it with the input alpha. For spill suppression, projecting the pixel chroma onto the key direction, and only removing the color toward the key is needed.


    The core despilling equation will subtract a projected chroma value, ensuring Rec. 709 luminance is maintained by reconstructing RGB from the despilled chroma components and original luminance. Implementation will use a 1-pixel repeat cache for `key_pixel` to optimize performance, similarly to another function. Checking how the alpha channel is handled in the `apply_chroma_key` function is a crucial next step.


    The UI utilizes `ColorImage::from_rgba_unmultiplied` which blends based on alpha; thus, the alpha channel needs to be correctly set for transparency. Investigating the relevant engine code reveals `Timeline::chroma_key`, `EditCommand::SetChromaKey`, and `mix::set_chroma_key` structures are already in place, simplifying the integration. The UI exposes chroma key functionality via a menu item and window with activation, presets, color selection, and sliders for parameter adjustments.

   - [otio.rs](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_engine/src/otio.rs): [`otio::export`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_engine/src/otio.rs#L97-L299), [`otio::parse`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_engine/src/otio.rs#L317-L441) e [`otio::import_into_timeline`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_engine/src/otio.rs#L670-L766) compatíveis com `Timeline.1`, `Stack.1`, `Track.1`, `Clip.1`/`Clip.2`, `Gap.1`, `Transition.1` (crossfades), `LinearTimeWarp.1` (time-stretch) e `Marker.2` (marcadores e regiões), integrados ao menu **Arquivo** em [app.rs](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_ui/src/app.rs#L600-L664) como um único passo de desfazer.

4. **Chaveamento de croma / Chroma Key em Rust puro (`54c7414`)**
   - [chroma.rs (common)](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_common/src/chroma.rs) & [chroma.rs (video)](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_video/src/chroma.rs): estrutura [`ChromaKey`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_common/src/chroma.rs#L8-L20) e [`apply_chroma_key`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_video/src/chroma.rs#L9-L36) com recorte no plano de crominância Rec. 709 `(Cb, Cr)` para tela verde/azul ou cor arbitrária, suavização de borda (*smoothstep*), supressão de *spill* preservando luminância, visualização de máscara P&B (*matte*), comando reversível [`EditCommand::SetChromaKey`](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_engine/src/command.rs#L105-L108) e janela **Ferramentas → Chaveamento de croma (Chroma Key)…** em [app.rs](file:///c:/Users/paulomatos/Documents/GitHub/neoveg/crates/neoveg_ui/src/app.rs#L3990-L4086).
